This skill covers reading gravitational wave frame (GWF) files and conditioning the data.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pycbc lalsuite\n```\n\n## Reading GWF Files\n\n### Basic Usage\n```python\nfrom pycbc.frame import read_frame\n\n# Read data from a GWF file\nstrain = read_frame('path/to/file.gwf', 'CHANNEL_NAME')\n\n# strain is a TimeSeries object\nprint(f\"Sample rate: {strain.sample_rate} Hz\")\nprint(f\"Duration: {strain.duration} seconds\")\nprint(f\"Start time: {strain.start_time}\")\n```\n\n### TimeSeries Object\nThe `TimeSeries` object contains:\n- `sample_rate`: Sampling frequency in Hz\n- `duration`: Length of the time series in seconds\n- `delta_t`: Time between samples (1/sample_rate)\n- `start_time`: GPS start time\n\n## Data Conditioning\n\nConditioning raw detector data typically involves:\n1. Resampling to a target sample rate\n2. High-pass filtering to remove low-frequency noise\n3. Band-pass filtering to focus on relevant frequency range\n\n### Resampling\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import resample_to_delta_t\n\n# Resample to 2048 Hz (delta_t = 1/2048)\nstrain_resampled = resample_to_delta_t(strain, 1.0/2048)\n```\n\n### High-pass Filtering\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import highpass\n\n# Apply high-pass filter at 15 Hz\nstrain_filtered = highpass(strain, frequency=15.0)\n```\n\n### Combined Conditioning\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import resample_to_delta_t, highpass\nfrom pycbc.psd import welch, interpolate\n\n# Resample and filter\nconditioned = resample_to_delta_t(strain, 1.0/2048)\nconditioned = highpass(conditioned, frequency=15.0)\n\n# Crop to remove filter artifacts (typically 2 seconds from each end)\nconditioned = conditioned.crop(2, 2)\n```\n\n## Power Spectral Density (PSD) Estimation\n\nThe PSD characterizes the noise in the detector:\n\n```python\nfrom pycbc.psd import welch, interpolate\n\n# Estimate PSD using Welch method\npsd = welch(strain, seg_len=4, seg_stride=2)\n\n# Interpolate PSD to match data sample rate\npsd = interpolate(psd, strain.delta_f)\n```\n\n## Common Channel Names\n- LIGO Hanford: `H1:GWOSC-4KHZ_R1_STRAIN` or `H1:TEST-STRAIN`\n- LIGO Livingston: `L1:GWOSC-4KHZ_R1_STRAIN` or `L1:TEST-STRAIN`\n- Virgo: `V1:GWOSC-4KHZ_R1_STRAIN`\n\n## References\n- PyCBC Documentation: https://pycbc.org/\n- LIGO Open Science Center: https://www.gw-openscience.org/\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":802,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":802,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"41cd69c1-298f-4363-bc76-1b5e6eb408e1","uuid":"ccd78c70-8030-4e69-9e1c-6ee27dd4c486"}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01XzW2iuH3fTHYw95rWqeSiY","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01K6iTrAGfAsKgYJ1sr6DSCM","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/pycbc-matched-filtering.md","content":"# PyCBC Matched Filtering for Gravitational Wave Detection\n\n## Overview\nMatched filtering is the optimal detection technique for known signal shapes in Gaussian noise. For gravitational waves, we cross-correlate detector data with theoretical waveform templates to find binary merger signals.\n\n## Concept\nThe matched filter SNR (signal-to-noise ratio) is computed by:\n1. Generating a template waveform\n2. Computing the overlap between data and template in frequency domain\n3. Normalizing by the noise power spectral density (PSD)\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pycbc\n```\n\n## Basic Matched Filter\n\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import matched_filter\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_fd_waveform\nfrom pycbc.psd import aLIGOZeroDetHighPower\n\n# Generate a template waveform\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\",\n                         mass1=30,\n                         mass2=30,\n                         delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n                         f_lower=20.0)\n\n# Compute matched filter SNR\nsnr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n\n# Find peak SNR\npeak_time = snr.abs_arg_max()\npeak_snr = abs(snr[peak_time])\n\nprint(f\"Peak SNR: {peak_snr:.2f} at time {peak_time}\")\n```\n\n## Key Parameters\n\n### matched_filter function\n- `template`: Template waveform (FrequencySeries)\n- `data`: Detector strain data (TimeSeries or FrequencySeries)\n- `psd`: Power spectral density (FrequencySeries)\n- `low_frequency_cutoff`: Minimum frequency for integration (Hz)\n- `high_frequency_cutoff`: Maximum frequency (optional)\n\n### Return Value\nReturns a TimeSeries of complex SNR values over time. The absolute value gives the SNR magnitude.\n\n## Finding Peaks\n\n```python\n# Get time of maximum SNR\npeak_time = snr.abs_arg_max()\n\n# Get SNR value at peak\npeak_snr = abs(snr[peak_time])\n\n# Get SNR time series as array\nsnr_array = abs(snr.numpy())\nmax_snr = snr_array.max()\n```\n\n## Grid Search Over Parameters\n\nTo search for unknown source parameters, perform matched filtering with multiple templates:\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\nbest_snr = 0\nbest_params = None\n\n# Grid search over mass parameters\nfor m1 in np.arange(10, 41, 1):\n    for m2 in np.arange(10, 41, 1):\n        if m2 > m1:  # Convention: m1 >= m2\n            continue\n\n        # Generate template\n        hp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\",\n                                 mass1=m1,\n                                 mass2=m2,\n                                 delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n                                 f_lower=20.0)\n\n        # Matched filter\n        snr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n\n        # Check if this is the best so far\n        peak_snr = abs(snr).max()\n        if peak_snr > best_snr:\n            best_snr = peak_snr\n            best_params = {'m1': m1, 'm2': m2}\n\nprint(f\"Best match: m1={best_params['m1']}, m2={best_params['m2']}, SNR={best_snr:.2f}\")\n```\n\n## Memory Management\n\nFor large grid searches, consider:\n```python\n# Resize template to match data length\nhp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n\n# Or convert data to frequency domain once\nfrom pycbc.types import FrequencySeries\nstrain_fd = strain.to_frequencyseries()\n```\n\n## SNR Threshold\nTypical detection thresholds:\n- SNR > 8: Potential detection\n- SNR > 12: High confidence\n- SNR > 5.5: Used with additional tests\n\n## References\n- Matched Filtering Theory: Allen et al. (2012), PRD 85, 122006\n- PyCBC Matched Filter: https://pycbc.org/pycbc/latest/html/pycbc.filter.html\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":802,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":802,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"41cd69c1-298f-4363-bc76-1b5e6eb408e1","uuid":"465004e8-7286-4b96-9a73-1cc2ea18c4aa"}

Different approximants have different accuracy, computational cost, and applicable parameter ranges.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pycbc lalsuite\n```\n\n## Waveform Generation\n\n### Frequency Domain Waveforms\n```python\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_fd_waveform\n\n# Generate frequency-domain waveform\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(\n    approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\",\n    mass1=36,              # Primary mass (solar masses)\n    mass2=29,              # Secondary mass (solar masses)\n    delta_f=1.0/32,        # Frequency resolution (Hz)\n    f_lower=20.0,          # Starting frequency (Hz)\n    distance=410,          # Luminosity distance (Mpc)\n    inclination=0,         # Inclination angle (radians)\n    spin1z=0,              # Primary spin (z-component)\n    spin2z=0               # Secondary spin (z-component)\n)\n\n# hp: plus polarization, hc: cross polarization\n```\n\n### Time Domain Waveforms\n```python\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_td_waveform\n\nhp, hc = get_td_waveform(\n    approximant=\"SEOBNRv4\",\n    mass1=36,\n    mass2=29,\n    delta_t=1.0/2048,      # Time step (seconds)\n    f_lower=20.0\n)\n```\n\n## Common Approximants\n\n### IMRPhenomD\n- **Type**: Phenomenological frequency-domain model\n- **Coverage**: Inspiral-Merger-Ringdown (IMR)\n- **Spins**: Aligned spins only\n- **Speed**: Very fast\n- **Use case**: Quick searches, high-mass systems\n```python\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\", mass1=30, mass2=25,\n                         delta_f=1/32, f_lower=20)\n```\n\n### SEOBNRv4 / SEOBNRv4_opt\n- **Type**: Effective-one-body model\n- **Coverage**: Inspiral-Merger-Ringdown\n- **Spins**: Aligned spins\n- **Speed**: Fast (especially _opt version)\n- **Accuracy**: High accuracy, calibrated to numerical relativity\n- **Use case**: Parameter estimation, aligned-spin systems\n```python\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=\"SEOBNRv4_opt\", mass1=30, mass2=25,\n                         delta_f=1/32, f_lower=20)\n```\n\n### TaylorT4\n- **Type**: Post-Newtonian time-domain model\n- **Coverage**: Inspiral only (no merger/ringdown)\n- **Spins**: Precessing spins supported\n- **Speed**: Slower\n- **Use case**: Low-mass systems, long inspirals, binary neutron stars\n```python\nhp, hc = get_td_waveform(approximant=\"TaylorT4\", mass1=1.4, mass2=1.4,\n                         delta_t=1/2048, f_lower=20)\n# Convert to frequency domain if needed\nhp_fd = hp.to_frequencyseries(delta_f=1/32)\n```\n\n### TaylorF2\n- **Type**: Post-Newtonian frequency-domain\n- **Coverage**: Inspiral only\n- **Speed**: Very fast\n- **Use case**: Quick inspiral-only analyses\n\n## Key Parameters\n\n### Masses\n- `mass1`, `mass2`: Component masses in solar masses (M☉)\n- Convention: mass1 >= mass2\n- Total mass: M_total = mass1 + mass2\n- Chirp mass: M_chirp = (mass1 * mass2)^(3/5) / M_total^(1/5)\n\n### Spins\n- `spin1z`, `spin2z`: Dimensionless spin (-1 to 1)\n- Aligned with orbital angular momentum\n- Positive: aligned, Negative: anti-aligned\n\n### Other Parameters\n- `delta_f`: Frequency spacing (Hz) - typically 1/duration\n- `delta_t`: Time spacing (s) - typically 1/sample_rate\n- `f_lower`: Starting frequency (Hz) - usually 15-30 Hz for LIGO\n- `f_final`: Ending frequency (optional)\n- `distance`: Luminosity distance (Mpc)\n- `inclination`: Viewing angle (0 = face-on, π/2 = edge-on)\n\n## Checking Available Approximants\n\n```python\nfrom pycbc.waveform import print_fd_approximants, print_td_approximants\n\n# List all frequency-domain approximants\nprint_fd_approximants()\n\n# List all time-domain approximants\nprint_td_approximants()\n```\n\n## Waveform Properties\n\n```python\n# Access waveform properties\nprint(f\"Sample rate: {hp.delta_f} Hz\")\nprint(f\"Length: {len(hp)} samples\")\nprint(f\"Duration: {len(hp) * hp.delta_f} seconds\")\n\n# Convert between domains\nhp_fd = hp_td.to_frequencyseries()\nhp_td = hp_fd.to_timeseries()\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Choose appropriate approximant**:\n   - High masses (> 10 M☉): IMRPhenomD, SEOBNRv4_opt\n   - Low masses (< 3 M☉): TaylorF2, TaylorT4\n   - Quick searches: IMRPhenomD\n   - Accurate modeling: SEOBNRv4_opt\n\n2. **Mass ordering**: Always use mass1 >= mass2\n\n3. **Frequency resolution**: Match data's delta_f\n\n4. **Starting frequency**: Higher f_lower = faster computation\n\n## References\n- PyCBC Waveform Documentation: https://pycbc.org/pycbc/latest/html/pycbc.waveform.html\n- LALSimulation: Data Acquisition\n```python\nfrom pycbc.frame import read_frame\n\n# Read gravitational wave frame file\nstrain = read_frame('data.gwf', 'H1:TEST-STRAIN')\n```\n\n### 2. Data Conditioning\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import resample_to_delta_t, highpass\n\n# Resample to target rate (e.g., 2048 Hz)\nstrain = resample_to_delta_t(strain, 1.0/2048)\n\n# High-pass filter to remove low-frequency noise\nstrain = highpass(strain, frequency=15.0)\n\n# Crop edges to remove filter transients\nstrain = strain.crop(2, 2)\n```\n\n### 3. PSD Estimation\n```python\nfrom pycbc.psd import welch, interpolate\n\n# Estimate noise power spectral density\npsd = welch(strain, seg_len=4, seg_stride=2)\n\n# Interpolate to match strain frequency resolution\npsd = interpolate(psd, strain.delta_f)\n```\n\n### 4. Template Generation\n```python\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_fd_waveform\n\n# Generate template waveform\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(\n    approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\",\n    mass1=35,\n    mass2=30,\n    delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n    f_lower=20.0\n)\n\n# Resize to match data length\nhp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n```\n\n### 5. Matched Filtering\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import matched_filter, sigma\n\n# Perform matched filtering\nsnr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n\n# Normalize (optional, for sigma-based SNR)\n# template_sigma = sigma(hp, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n# snr = snr / template_sigma\n\n# Find peak\npeak_time = snr.abs_arg_max()\npeak_snr = abs(snr[peak_time])\n```\n\n### 6. Grid Search\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\nresults = []\n\nfor approximant in [\"IMRPhenomD\", \"SEOBNRv4_opt\", \"TaylorT4\"]:\n    best_snr = 0\n    best_m1, best_m2 = 0, 0\n\n    for m1 in range(10, 41):\n        for m2 in range(10, m1+1):  # m2 <= m1\n            # Generate template\n            hp, hc = get_fd_waveform(\n                approximant=approximant,\n                mass1=m1,\n                mass2=m2,\n                delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n                f_lower=20.0\n            )\n            hp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n\n            # Matched filter\n            snr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n            peak_snr = abs(snr).max()\n\n            if peak_snr > best_snr:\n                best_snr = peak_snr\n                best_m1, best_m2 = m1, m2\n\n    results.append({\n        'approximant': approximant,\n        'snr': best_snr,\n        'mass1': best_m1,\n        'mass2': best_m2,\n        'total_mass': best_m1 + best_m2\n    })\n```\n\n### 7. Save Results\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\ndf = pd.DataFrame(results)\ndf = df[['approximant', 'snr', 'total_mass']]\ndf.to_csv('results.csv', index=False)\n```\n\n## Complete Example\n\n```python\nfrom pycbc.frame import read_frame\nfrom pycbc.filter import resample_to_delta_t, highpass, matched_filter\nfrom pycbc.psd import welch, interpolate\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_fd_waveform\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# 1. Load data\nstrain = read_frame('data.gwf', 'H1:TEST-STRAIN')\n\n# 2. Condition data\nstrain = resample_to_delta_t(strain, 1.0/2048)\nstrain = highpass(strain, frequency=15.0)\nstrain = strain.crop(2, 2)\n\n# 3. Estimate PSD\npsd = welch(strain, seg_len=4, seg_stride=2)\npsd = interpolate(psd, strain.delta_f)\n\n# 4. Grid search\nresults = []\nfor approx in [\"IMRPhenomD\", \"SEOBNRv4_opt\", \"TaylorT4\"]:\n    best_snr = 0\n    best_params = {}\n\n    for m1 in range(10, 41):\n        for m2 in range(10, m1+1):\n            hp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=approx, mass1=m1, mass2=m2,\n                                     delta_f=strain.delta_f, f_lower=20.0)\n            hp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n\n            snr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n            peak = abs(snr).max()\n\n            if peak > best_snr:\n                best_snr = peak\n                best_params = {'m1': m1, 'm2': m2}\n\n    results.append({\n        'approximant': approx,\n        'snr': best_snr,\n        'total_mass': best_params['m1'] + best_params['m2']\n    })\n\n# 5. Save\npd.DataFrame(results).to_csv('detection_results.csv', index=False)\n```\n\n## Performance Tips\n\n1. **Convert to frequency domain once**: If doing multiple matched filters on same data\n2. **Resize templates**: Match data length to avoid errors\n3. **Choose appropriate f_lower**: Higher = faster but may miss signal\n4. **Use optimized approximants**: SEOBNRv4_opt vs SEOBNRv4\n\n## Common Issues\n\n- **Length mismatch**: Ensure template and data have compatible lengths\n- **Memory errors**: Process in batches for large grids\n- **Low SNR**: Check PSD estimation, frequency cutoffs, and template parameters\n\n## References\n- LIGO Scientific Collaboration tutorials: https://www.gw-openscience.org/tutorials/\n- PyCBC tutorials: https://github.com/gwastro/pycbc-tutorials\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":802,"cache_read_input_tokens":18094,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":802,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"41cd69c1-298f-4363-bc76-1b5e6eb408e1","uuid":"d0472962-590a-49e3-9755-957f9da1c78c"}

This skill covers reading gravitational wave frame (GWF) files and conditioning the data.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pycbc lalsuite\n```\n\n## Reading GWF Files\n\n### Basic Usage\n```python\nfrom pycbc.frame import read_frame\n\n# Read data from a GWF file\nstrain = read_frame('path/to/file.gwf', 'CHANNEL_NAME')\n\n# strain is a TimeSeries object\nprint(f\"Sample rate: {strain.sample_rate} Hz\")\nprint(f\"Duration: {strain.duration} seconds\")\nprint(f\"Start time: {strain.start_time}\")\n```\n\n### TimeSeries Object\nThe `TimeSeries` object contains:\n- `sample_rate`: Sampling frequency in Hz\n- `duration`: Length of the time series in seconds\n- `delta_t`: Time between samples (1/sample_rate)\n- `start_time`: GPS start time\n\n## Data Conditioning\n\nConditioning raw detector data typically involves:\n1. Resampling to a target sample rate\n2. High-pass filtering to remove low-frequency noise\n3. Band-pass filtering to focus on relevant frequency range\n\n### Resampling\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import resample_to_delta_t\n\n# Resample to 2048 Hz (delta_t = 1/2048)\nstrain_resampled = resample_to_delta_t(strain, 1.0/2048)\n```\n\n### High-pass Filtering\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import highpass\n\n# Apply high-pass filter at 15 Hz\nstrain_filtered = highpass(strain, frequency=15.0)\n```\n\n### Combined Conditioning\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import resample_to_delta_t, highpass\nfrom pycbc.psd import welch, interpolate\n\n# Resample and filter\nconditioned = resample_to_delta_t(strain, 1.0/2048)\nconditioned = highpass(conditioned, frequency=15.0)\n\n# Crop to remove filter artifacts (typically 2 seconds from each end)\nconditioned = conditioned.crop(2, 2)\n```\n\n## Power Spectral Density (PSD) Estimation\n\nThe PSD characterizes the noise in the detector:\n\n```python\nfrom pycbc.psd import welch, interpolate\n\n# Estimate PSD using Welch method\npsd = welch(strain, seg_len=4, seg_stride=2)\n\n# Interpolate PSD to match data sample rate\npsd = interpolate(psd, strain.delta_f)\n```\n\n## Common Channel Names\n- LIGO Hanford: `H1:GWOSC-4KHZ_R1_STRAIN` or `H1:TEST-STRAIN`\n- LIGO Livingston: `L1:GWOSC-4KHZ_R1_STRAIN` or `L1:TEST-STRAIN`\n- Virgo: `V1:GWOSC-4KHZ_R1_STRAIN`\n\n## References\n- PyCBC Documentation: https://pycbc.org/\n- LIGO Open Science Center: https://www.gw-openscience.org/\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

For gravitational waves, we cross-correlate detector data with theoretical waveform templates to find binary merger signals.\n\n## Concept\nThe matched filter SNR (signal-to-noise ratio) is computed by:\n1. Generating a template waveform\n2. Computing the overlap between data and template in frequency domain\n3. Normalizing by the noise power spectral density (PSD)\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pycbc\n```\n\n## Basic Matched Filter\n\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import matched_filter\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_fd_waveform\nfrom pycbc.psd import aLIGOZeroDetHighPower\n\n# Generate a template waveform\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\",\n                         mass1=30,\n                         mass2=30,\n                         delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n                         f_lower=20.0)\n\n# Compute matched filter SNR\nsnr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n\n# Find peak SNR\npeak_time = snr.abs_arg_max()\npeak_snr = abs(snr[peak_time])\n\nprint(f\"Peak SNR: {peak_snr:.2f} at time {peak_time}\")\n```\n\n## Key Parameters\n\n### matched_filter function\n- `template`: Template waveform (FrequencySeries)\n- `data`: Detector strain data (TimeSeries or FrequencySeries)\n- `psd`: Power spectral density (FrequencySeries)\n- `low_frequency_cutoff`: Minimum frequency for integration (Hz)\n- `high_frequency_cutoff`: Maximum frequency (optional)\n\n### Return Value\nReturns a TimeSeries of complex SNR values over time. The absolute value gives the SNR magnitude.\n\n## Finding Peaks\n\n```python\n# Get time of maximum SNR\npeak_time = snr.abs_arg_max()\n\n# Get SNR value at peak\npeak_snr = abs(snr[peak_time])\n\n# Get SNR time series as array\nsnr_array = abs(snr.numpy())\nmax_snr = snr_array.max()\n```\n\n## Grid Search Over Parameters\n\nTo search for unknown source parameters, perform matched filtering with multiple templates:\n\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\nbest_snr = 0\nbest_params = None\n\n# Grid search over mass parameters\nfor m1 in np.arange(10, 41, 1):\n    for m2 in np.arange(10, 41, 1):\n        if m2 > m1:  # Convention: m1 >= m2\n            continue\n\n        # Generate template\n        hp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\",\n                                 mass1=m1,\n                                 mass2=m2,\n                                 delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n                                 f_lower=20.0)\n\n        # Matched filter\n        snr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n\n        # Check if this is the best so far\n        peak_snr = abs(snr).max()\n        if peak_snr > best_snr:\n            best_snr = peak_snr\n            best_params = {'m1': m1, 'm2': m2}\n\nprint(f\"Best match: m1={best_params['m1']}, m2={best_params['m2']}, SNR={best_snr:.2f}\")\n```\n\n## Memory Management\n\nFor large grid searches, consider:\n```python\n# Resize template to match data length\nhp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n\n# Or convert data to frequency domain once\nfrom pycbc.types import FrequencySeries\nstrain_fd = strain.to_frequencyseries()\n```\n\n## SNR Threshold\nTypical detection thresholds:\n- SNR > 8: Potential detection\n- SNR > 12: High confidence\n- SNR > 5.5: Used with additional tests\n\n## References\n- Matched Filtering Theory: Allen et al. (2012), PRD 85, 122006\n- PyCBC Matched Filter: https://pycbc.org/pycbc/latest/html/pycbc.filter.html\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Different approximants have different accuracy, computational cost, and applicable parameter ranges.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pycbc lalsuite\n```\n\n## Waveform Generation\n\n### Frequency Domain Waveforms\n```python\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_fd_waveform\n\n# Generate frequency-domain waveform\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(\n    approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\",\n    mass1=36,              # Primary mass (solar masses)\n    mass2=29,              # Secondary mass (solar masses)\n    delta_f=1.0/32,        # Frequency resolution (Hz)\n    f_lower=20.0,          # Starting frequency (Hz)\n    distance=410,          # Luminosity distance (Mpc)\n    inclination=0,         # Inclination angle (radians)\n    spin1z=0,              # Primary spin (z-component)\n    spin2z=0               # Secondary spin (z-component)\n)\n\n# hp: plus polarization, hc: cross polarization\n```\n\n### Time Domain Waveforms\n```python\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_td_waveform\n\nhp, hc = get_td_waveform(\n    approximant=\"SEOBNRv4\",\n    mass1=36,\n    mass2=29,\n    delta_t=1.0/2048,      # Time step (seconds)\n    f_lower=20.0\n)\n```\n\n## Common Approximants\n\n### IMRPhenomD\n- **Type**: Phenomenological frequency-domain model\n- **Coverage**: Inspiral-Merger-Ringdown (IMR)\n- **Spins**: Aligned spins only\n- **Speed**: Very fast\n- **Use case**: Quick searches, high-mass systems\n```python\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\", mass1=30, mass2=25,\n                         delta_f=1/32, f_lower=20)\n```\n\n### SEOBNRv4 / SEOBNRv4_opt\n- **Type**: Effective-one-body model\n- **Coverage**: Inspiral-Merger-Ringdown\n- **Spins**: Aligned spins\n- **Speed**: Fast (especially _opt version)\n- **Accuracy**: High accuracy, calibrated to numerical relativity\n- **Use case**: Parameter estimation, aligned-spin systems\n```python\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=\"SEOBNRv4_opt\", mass1=30, mass2=25,\n                         delta_f=1/32, f_lower=20)\n```\n\n### TaylorT4\n- **Type**: Post-Newtonian time-domain model\n- **Coverage**: Inspiral only (no merger/ringdown)\n- **Spins**: Precessing spins supported\n- **Speed**: Slower\n- **Use case**: Low-mass systems, long inspirals, binary neutron stars\n```python\nhp, hc = get_td_waveform(approximant=\"TaylorT4\", mass1=1.4, mass2=1.4,\n                         delta_t=1/2048, f_lower=20)\n# Convert to frequency domain if needed\nhp_fd = hp.to_frequencyseries(delta_f=1/32)\n```\n\n### TaylorF2\n- **Type**: Post-Newtonian frequency-domain\n- **Coverage**: Inspiral only\n- **Speed**: Very fast\n- **Use case**: Quick inspiral-only analyses\n\n## Key Parameters\n\n### Masses\n- `mass1`, `mass2`: Component masses in solar masses (M☉)\n- Convention: mass1 >= mass2\n- Total mass: M_total = mass1 + mass2\n- Chirp mass: M_chirp = (mass1 * mass2)^(3/5) / M_total^(1/5)\n\n### Spins\n- `spin1z`, `spin2z`: Dimensionless spin (-1 to 1)\n- Aligned with orbital angular momentum\n- Positive: aligned, Negative: anti-aligned\n\n### Other Parameters\n- `delta_f`: Frequency spacing (Hz) - typically 1/duration\n- `delta_t`: Time spacing (s) - typically 1/sample_rate\n- `f_lower`: Starting frequency (Hz) - usually 15-30 Hz for LIGO\n- `f_final`: Ending frequency (optional)\n- `distance`: Luminosity distance (Mpc)\n- `inclination`: Viewing angle (0 = face-on, π/2 = edge-on)\n\n## Checking Available Approximants\n\n```python\nfrom pycbc.waveform import print_fd_approximants, print_td_approximants\n\n# List all frequency-domain approximants\nprint_fd_approximants()\n\n# List all time-domain approximants\nprint_td_approximants()\n```\n\n## Waveform Properties\n\n```python\n# Access waveform properties\nprint(f\"Sample rate: {hp.delta_f} Hz\")\nprint(f\"Length: {len(hp)} samples\")\nprint(f\"Duration: {len(hp) * hp.delta_f} seconds\")\n\n# Convert between domains\nhp_fd = hp_td.to_frequencyseries()\nhp_td = hp_fd.to_timeseries()\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Choose appropriate approximant**:\n   - High masses (> 10 M☉): IMRPhenomD, SEOBNRv4_opt\n   - Low masses (< 3 M☉): TaylorF2, TaylorT4\n   - Quick searches: IMRPhenomD\n   - Accurate modeling: SEOBNRv4_opt\n\n2. **Mass ordering**: Always use mass1 >= mass2\n\n3. **Frequency resolution**: Match data's delta_f\n\n4. **Starting frequency**: Higher f_lower = faster computation\n\n## References\n- PyCBC Waveform Documentation: https://pycbc.org/pycbc/latest/html/pycbc.waveform.html\n- LALSimulation: Data Acquisition\n```python\nfrom pycbc.frame import read_frame\n\n# Read gravitational wave frame file\nstrain = read_frame('data.gwf', 'H1:TEST-STRAIN')\n```\n\n### 2. Data Conditioning\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import resample_to_delta_t, highpass\n\n# Resample to target rate (e.g., 2048 Hz)\nstrain = resample_to_delta_t(strain, 1.0/2048)\n\n# High-pass filter to remove low-frequency noise\nstrain = highpass(strain, frequency=15.0)\n\n# Crop edges to remove filter transients\nstrain = strain.crop(2, 2)\n```\n\n### 3. PSD Estimation\n```python\nfrom pycbc.psd import welch, interpolate\n\n# Estimate noise power spectral density\npsd = welch(strain, seg_len=4, seg_stride=2)\n\n# Interpolate to match strain frequency resolution\npsd = interpolate(psd, strain.delta_f)\n```\n\n### 4. Template Generation\n```python\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_fd_waveform\n\n# Generate template waveform\nhp, hc = get_fd_waveform(\n    approximant=\"IMRPhenomD\",\n    mass1=35,\n    mass2=30,\n    delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n    f_lower=20.0\n)\n\n# Resize to match data length\nhp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n```\n\n### 5. Matched Filtering\n```python\nfrom pycbc.filter import matched_filter, sigma\n\n# Perform matched filtering\nsnr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n\n# Normalize (optional, for sigma-based SNR)\n# template_sigma = sigma(hp, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n# snr = snr / template_sigma\n\n# Find peak\npeak_time = snr.abs_arg_max()\npeak_snr = abs(snr[peak_time])\n```\n\n### 6. Grid Search\n```python\nimport numpy as np\n\nresults = []\n\nfor approximant in [\"IMRPhenomD\", \"SEOBNRv4_opt\", \"TaylorT4\"]:\n    best_snr = 0\n    best_m1, best_m2 = 0, 0\n\n    for m1 in range(10, 41):\n        for m2 in range(10, m1+1):  # m2 <= m1\n            # Generate template\n            hp, hc = get_fd_waveform(\n                approximant=approximant,\n                mass1=m1,\n                mass2=m2,\n                delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n                f_lower=20.0\n            )\n            hp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n\n            # Matched filter\n            snr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n            peak_snr = abs(snr).max()\n\n            if peak_snr > best_snr:\n                best_snr = peak_snr\n                best_m1, best_m2 = m1, m2\n\n    results.append({\n        'approximant': approximant,\n        'snr': best_snr,\n        'mass1': best_m1,\n        'mass2': best_m2,\n        'total_mass': best_m1 + best_m2\n    })\n```\n\n### 7. Save Results\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\ndf = pd.DataFrame(results)\ndf = df[['approximant', 'snr', 'total_mass']]\ndf.to_csv('results.csv', index=False)\n```\n\n## Complete Example\n\n```python\nfrom pycbc.frame import read_frame\nfrom pycbc.filter import resample_to_delta_t, highpass, matched_filter\nfrom pycbc.psd import welch, interpolate\nfrom pycbc.waveform import get_fd_waveform\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# 1. Estimate PSD\npsd = welch(strain, seg_len=4, seg_stride=2)\npsd = interpolate(psd, strain.delta_f)\n\n# 4. Grid search\nresults = []\nfor approx in [\"IMRPhenomD\", \"SEOBNRv4_opt\", \"TaylorT4\"]:\n    best_snr = 0\n    best_params = {}\n\n    for m1 in range(10, 41):\n        for m2 in range(10, m1+1):\n            hp, hc = get_fd_waveform(approximant=approx, mass1=m1, mass2=m2,\n                                     delta_f=strain.delta_f, f_lower=20.0)\n            hp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n\n            snr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=20.0)\n            peak = abs(snr).max()\n\n            if peak > best_snr:\n                best_snr = peak\n                best_params = {'m1': m1, 'm2': m2}\n\n    results.append({\n        'approximant': approx,\n        'snr': best_snr,\n        'total_mass': best_params['m1'] + best_params['m2']\n    })\n\n# 5. Save\npd.DataFrame(results).to_csv('detection_results.csv', index=False)\n```\n\n## Performance Tips\n\n1. **Convert to frequency domain once**: If doing multiple matched filters on same data\n2. **Resize templates**: Match data length to avoid errors\n3. **Choose appropriate f_lower**: Higher = faster but may miss signal\n4. **Use optimized approximants**: SEOBNRv4_opt vs SEOBNRv4\n\n## Common Issues\n\n- **Length mismatch**: Ensure template and data have compatible lengths\n- **Memory errors**: Process in batches for large grids\n- **Low SNR**: Check PSD estimation, frequency cutoffs, and template parameters\n\n## References\n- LIGO Scientific Collaboration tutorials: https://www.gw-openscience.org/tutorials/\n- PyCBC tutorials: https://github.com/gwastro/pycbc-tutorials\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Resample to target rate\n    print(f\"Resampling to {sample_rate} Hz...\")\n    strain = resample_to_delta_t(strain, 1.0/sample_rate)\n\n    # High-pass filter\n    print(f\"Applying high-pass filter at {highpass_freq} Hz...\")\n    strain = highpass(strain, frequency=highpass_freq)\n\n    # Crop edges to remove filter artifacts\n    print(\"Cropping edges (2 seconds from each side)...\")\n    strain = strain.crop(2, 2)\n\n    print(f\"Conditioned data: {len(strain)} samples, {strain.duration:.2f} seconds\")\n\n    return strain\n\ndef estimate_psd(strain, seg_len=4, seg_stride=2):\n    \"\"\"\n    Estimate power spectral density using Welch method\n\n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    strain : TimeSeries\n        Input strain data\n    seg_len : float\n        Length of segments for Welch method (seconds)\n    seg_stride : float\n        Stride between segments (seconds)\n\n    Returns:\n    --------\n    psd : FrequencySeries\n        Power spectral density\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"Estimating PSD (seg_len={seg_len}s, seg_stride={seg_stride}s)...\")\n    psd = welch(strain, seg_len=seg_len, seg_stride=seg_stride)\n\n    # Interpolate to match strain frequency resolution\n    psd = interpolate(psd, strain.delta_f)\n\n    print(f\"PSD estimated: {len(psd)} frequency bins\")\n\n    return psd\n\ndef grid_search_masses(strain, psd, approximant, mass_range=(10, 41), f_lower=20.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Perform grid search over mass parameters\n\n    Parameters:\n    -----------\n    strain : TimeSeries\n        Detector strain data\n    psd : FrequencySeries\n        Power spectral density\n    approximant : str\n        Waveform approximant name\n    mass_range : tuple\n        (min_mass, max_mass) in solar masses\n    f_lower : float\n        Lower frequency cutoff in Hz\n\n    Returns:\n    --------\n    dict : Best match parameters and SNR\n    \"\"\"\n    print(f\"\\nGrid search for {approximant}\")\n    print(f\"Mass range: {mass_range[0]}-{mass_range[1]-1} solar masses\")\n\n    best_snr = 0\n    best_m1, best_m2 = 0, 0\n    total_templates = 0\n\n    # Grid search over masses (integer steps)\n    for m1 in range(mass_range[0], mass_range[1]):\n        for m2 in range(mass_range[0], m1+1):  # m2 <= m1 convention\n            total_templates += 1\n\n            try:\n                # Generate template waveform\n                hp, hc = get_fd_waveform(\n                    approximant=approximant,\n                    mass1=m1,\n                    mass2=m2,\n                    delta_f=strain.delta_f,\n                    f_lower=f_lower\n                )\n\n                # Resize template to match data length\n                hp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n\n                # Perform matched filtering\n                snr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=f_lower)\n\n                # Find peak SNR\n                peak_snr = abs(snr).max()\n\n                # Update best match\n                if peak_snr > best_snr:\n                    best_snr = peak_snr\n                    best_m1, best_m2 = m1, m2\n                    print(f\"  New best: m1={m1}, m2={m2}, SNR={peak_snr:.2f}\")\n\n            except Exception as e:\n                # Some mass combinations may fail for certain approximants\n                print(f\"  Warning: Failed for m1={m1}, m2={m2}: {str(e)}\")\n                continue\n\n    result = {\n        'approximant': approximant,\n        'mass1': best_m1,\n        'mass2': best_m2,\n        'total_mass': best_m1 + best_m2,\n        'snr': best_snr\n    }\n\n    print(f\"\\nBest match for {approximant}:\")\n    print(f\"  Mass1: {best_m1} M☉\")\n    print(f\"  Mass2: {best_m2} M☉\")\n    print(f\"  Total mass: {best_m1 + best_m2} M☉\")\n    print(f\"  SNR: {best_snr:.2f}\")\n    print(f\"  Templates tested: {total_templates}\")\n\n    return result\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Main analysis pipeline\"\"\"\n\n    # Resize template to match data length\n                hp.resize(len(strain) // 2 + 1)\n\n                # Perform matched filtering\n                snr = matched_filter(hp, strain, psd=psd, low_frequency_cutoff=f_lower)\n\n                #
